````math
|  | 1.6 | 74.3 | 3.73 | 70.4 | 4.41 |
|  | 2.4 | 38.6 | 3.59 | 37.7 | 3.69 |
|  | 3.2 | 20.0 | 2.74 | 20.0 | 4.04 |
|  | 4.0 | 11.2 | 2.65 | 11.8 | 2.55 |
| Scenario 4 |  |  |  |  |  |
|  | 0.8 | 83.6 | 4.12 | 81.0 | 4.43 |
|  | 1.6 | 68.1 | 5.86 | 66.9 | 6.53 |
|  | 2.4 | 33.8 | 4.58 | 28.6 | 3.69 |
|  | 3.2 | 19.4 | 4.82 | 18.4 | 4.97 |
|  | 4.0 | 13.2 | 5.13 | 13.1 | 4.56 |

Table 4: The MSEs for TPRs of different methods under four different
scenarios with default priors (All values in units of
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723}`$).
\#Anns represents the number of annotations for each recording.

In general, all of the models’ performances improve in terms of both
coverage and MSEs of annotators’ TPRs as the number of annotations per
recording increases. Comparing Base and DP-BMM, the latter has higher
coverages and lower MSEs when the occurrences of bird species are
correlated, indicating the DP Bernoulli mixture model’s advantage in
capturing and exploiting correlations among species. Comparing
Base-Hierarchical and DP-BMM-Hierarchical, the simpler model performs
better when the number of annotations is low even when occurrences of
species are correlated; hence, for inferring annotator ability scores,
an overly simple model may be preferred when training data are extremely
sparse. However, from Tables
[3](#S3.T3 "Table 3 ‣ 3.1.2 Assessment of species identification expertise ‣ 3.1 Simulation results ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[4](#S3.T4 "Table 4 ‣ 3.1.2 Assessment of species identification expertise ‣ 3.1 Simulation results ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
Base-Hierarchical/DP-BMM-Hierarchical have much better performance than
Base/DP-BMM in terms of both coverage and MSE across the scenarios
especially when the number of annotations is low. The latter ones could
be regarded as the limiting case of the former ones, where
$`\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathchar 8707}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28960}^{\mathchar 8707}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720`$.
From the sampling algorithms listed in Appendix
[A.3](#A1.SS3 "A.3 Posterior inference for DP-BMM-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
when the annotations are sparse and reasonable priors are adopted, the
additional hierarchical structure help pull
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$ towards the prior mean, thus
constraining the posterior samples of
$`\mathchar 28955\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}\right\delimiter 84054785`$
within a reasonable range. Additionally, the gap in the performances
between the two types of models in estimating annotators’ identification
expertise decreases as more annotations are collected.

According to the additional results in Appendix
[B.2](#A2.SS2 "B.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ Appendix B Additional results for simulation studies ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
Base-Hierarchical and DP-BMM-Hierarchical generally have significantly
better performances under different priors for annotators’ TPRs and
occurrence probabilities when few annotations are obtained, whereas Base
and DP-BMM are more sensitive to the prior specification in sparse data
cases. If somewhat unreasonable priors are chosen, performance of the
non-hierarchical models suffers even for moderately large numbers of
annotations.

### 3.2 Results for bird species annotations

We now present results for the Finnish Kerttu data using the prior
specification and evaluation setup described in Section
[2.8](#S2.SS8 "2.8 Application-specific prior specification and evaluation setup ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").

#### 3.2.1 Species identification

We first assess species identification performance. Figure
[2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.2.1 Species identification ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
displays the ROC curves for the five annotation aggregation methods.

Figure 2: ROC curves for five annotation aggregation methods. Colors
correspond to different aggregation methods. The AUCs of MV, Base,
Base-Hierarchical, DP-BMM and DP-BMM-Hierarchical are 0.849, 0.905,
0.903, 0.905 and 0.881, respectively.

As shown in Figure
[2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.2.1 Species identification ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
all of our models demonstrate significantly superior performances
compared with MV in terms of AUC. This remarkable improvement can be
primarily attributed to accounting for heterogeneous bird song
identification expertise among annotators. Upon closer examination of
Figure
[2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.2.1 Species identification ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
at a given FPR, Base, Base-Hierarchical and DP-BMM exhibit similar TPRs,
which are slightly higher than that of DP-BMM-Hierarchical. In our
problem, most annotators correctly indicate the absence of a bird
species in a recording. Then what we care about is whether annotators
can correctly identify the species when it vocalizes. In our current
data, Base/DP-BMM outperform the other three methods in terms of TPR.
Base-Hierarchical and MV have similar performances in terms of
sensitivity, whereas DP-BMM-Hierarchical seems to be the worst. When
annotations are sparse it is difficult to accurately estimate
annotators’ species-specific identification expertise under a highly
flexible model. However, we expect based on our simulations that
DP-BMM-Hierarchical’s relative performance will improve as additional
annotations become available.

In addition to predictive performance in inferring the species in terms
of AUC, we care about how well the models fit the data. For Bayesian
hierarchical models, we assess predictive loss using the Watanabe-Akaike
information criterion (WAIC) ([Watanabe and Opper, 2010](#bib.bib43)),
$`\text{WAIC}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\left\delimiter 67273472\text{lppd}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29040_{\text{WAIC}}\right\delimiter 84054785`$,
where lppd represents the log point-wise predictive density and
$`\mathchar 29040_{\text{WAIC}}`$ corresponds to a correction for
effective number of parameters to adjust for over-fitting; see [Gelman
et al. (2013)](#bib.bib13); [Gelman et al. (2014)](#bib.bib44).
Sometimes, an overly simple model will do just as well in terms of
prediction error but then predictive uncertainty is underestimated.
Therefore, we are interested in the accuracy of probabilistic
predictions, and we choose to use the Brier score (BS) to measure the
model calibration, with lower values indicating better calibration. All
models’ performances in terms of WAIC and BS are summarized in Table
[5](#S3.T5 "Table 5 ‣ 3.2.1 Species identification ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
Combining these metrics, we see a trade-off between model complexity and
predictive calibration. DP-BMM excels in fitting the data but falls
short in predictive calibration; conversely, Base-Hierarchical, while
not the best in terms of WAIC, offers the most reliable predictive
calibration. For our problem, Base-Hierarchical may be the most
appropriate model overall, balancing acceptable fit to the data with
superior calibration of uncertainty.

|  | Base | Base-Hierarchical | DP-BMM | DP-BMM-Hierarchical |
|----|----|----|----|----|
| lppd | -8120.59 | -8274.19 | -7719.85 | -11431.57 |
| $`\mathchar 29040_{\text{WAIC}}`$ | 15693.27 | 18464.95 | 14354.50 | 18979.46 |
| WAIC | 47627.73 | 53478.30 | 44148.72 | 60822.06 |
| BS | 0.0172 | 0.0163 | 0.0174 | 0.0165 |

Table 5: WAICs and Brier scores of four Bayesian hierarchical models,
which are computed based on posterior draws from Gibbs samplers.

#### 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise

In our problem, annotators are more likely to omit species that are
actually present in the recordings than to falsely identify species that
are not vocalizing in recordings. Consequently, given that our
annotators exhibit low FPRs, our primary focus is to assess their bird
song identification expertise based on their TPRs
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$’s. The posterior distributions of
each annotator’s $`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}`$ are provided in
Appendix
[D](#A4 "Appendix D Additional results for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
Our Markov chains appear to have converged given that the posterior
distributions obtained from three chains with widely different starting
points are quite similar for each model. We visualize the posterior
distribution of each annotator’s $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$
in Figures
[3](#S3.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[4](#S3.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
In Base-Hierarchical and DP-BMM-Hierarchical, we do not directly model
annotators’ overall TPRs or FPRs. Instead, we model their
species-specific TPRs
$`\mathchar 28955\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785`$’s
and FPRs
$`\mathchar 28955\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785`$’s.
However, here we use
$`\mathchar 28955\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785`$
and
$`\mathchar 28955\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785`$
to roughly represent their overall TPRs and FPRs.

(a) The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in Base.

(b) The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in
Base-Hierarchical.

Figure 3: The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in two models
that do not take into account correlations among bird species. Levels
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724`$
represent the different levels of annotators’ bird song identification
expertise. Level $`\mathchar 28724`$ corresponds to the highest skill
level, followed by decreasing levels of 3, 2, and 1 in that order.

(a) The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in DP-BMM.

(b) The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in
DP-BMM-Hierarchical.

Figure 4: The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in two models
that take into account correlations among bird species. Levels
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724`$
represent the different levels of annotators’ bird song identification
expertise. Level $`\mathchar 28724`$ corresponds to the highest skill
level, followed by decreasing levels of 3, 2, and 1 in that order.

While we do not have access to the ground truth of annotators’
identification expertise, we can utilize their self-reported Finnish
bird song identification levels as a reference to assess our approach.
To analyze the posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs and FPRs, we
used distinct colors to represent different skill levels and create
violin plots. Figures
[3](#S3.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[4](#S3.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
reveal that annotators with higher levels generally exhibit higher
estimated TPRs across all four models. This alignment with annotators’
self-assessments of their expertise is reassuring. Comparing Figures
[3](#S3.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[4](#S3.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
we observed that the posterior distributions of different annotators’
TPRs in Base and DP-BMM are quite similar, while the posterior
distributions of annotators’ TPRs from Base-Hierarchical and
DP-BMM-Hierarchical are also similar to each other. Accurately
characterizing species correlation seems to have little impact on
inferences on annotators’ identification expertise for these data.

Comparing two subplots in Figure
[3](#S3.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
or
[4](#S3.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ 3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
we notice that the estimated TPRs in
Base-Hierarchical/DP-BMM-Hierarchical exhibit more variability compared
with those in Base/DP-BMM. Specifically, some annotators with high TPRs
in Base/DP-BMM would get significantly lower TPRs in
Base-Hierarchical/DP-BMM-Hierarchical. Upon closer examination, we find
annotators who are knowledgeable about many species and have provided
limited annotations tend to have lower TPRs in the models with the
hierarchical structure of identification expertise. In the models
without the hierarchical structure, the posterior distributions of their
TPRs are dominated by informative priors because they contributed fewer
annotations compared with others. However, for the models with the
hierarchical structure, we noticed the estimated
$`\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28949}^{\mathchar 8707}`$ is considerably
larger than $`\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28949}`$, indicating that
species-specific TPRs are primarily influenced by the data likelihood.
Consequently, annotators who provided a higher proportion of
$`\mathchar 28720`$ will have much lower species-specific TPRs or
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 561\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8235\mathchar 561\delimiter 84054785`$
compared with other annotators. Furthermore, different from Section
[3.1](#S3.SS1 "3.1 Simulation results ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
where we only estimate annotators’ species-specific TPRs for species
annotators gave annotations to, here we also consider the
species-specific TPRs for species in their expertise sets mentioned in
Section
[2.5](#S2.SS5 "2.5 Hierarchical modeling of annotator expertise ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
For annotators whose pre-specified sets contain extensive species, the
presence of numerous relatively lower
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$’s
will lead to low $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$’s, and limited
annotations are not rich enough to reliably estimate their expertise in
the models with the complex hierarchical structure.

## 4 Discussion

Our research has provided significant insights into the complexities and
challenges inherent in combining annotations for bird songs, offering
valuable contributions to citizen science-based avian acoustics. We
explored Bayesian hierarchical models for species annotation, aiming to
improve accuracy of aggregated annotations by addressing the variability
in annotators’ identification expertise and the dependence among species
occurrences. Our main results show that i) annotation aggregation
through Bayesian hierarchical models outperforms the widely used
Majority Vote method and ii) the most flexible models are not
necessarily the best when applied on very sparse datasets which are
typical in citizen science projects.

One characteristic of our modeling framework is the consideration of
annotators’ varying expertise across different species. This variability
is crucial as it mirrors real-world scenarios where annotators may excel
in identifying certain species but struggle with others due to multiple
species having similar vocalizations or the expert being specialized in
a specific group of species. However, due to limited annotations, the
results in Section
[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
demonstrate that the additional modeling flexibility didn’t lead to
improved predictive performance or better fit to the data. Contrary to
our initial expectations, the most complex model DP-BMM-Hierarchical did
not consistently outperform simpler alternatives. Specifically, while
DP-BMM exhibited superior performance in fitting the data, its
predictive calibration suffered compared to Base-Hierarchical, which
offered a more reliable estimation of uncertainty and better
computational efficiency. This discrepancy highlights the need to
balance model complexity with practical utility in biomonitoring
applications.

Moving forward, our research opens up several promising avenues for
further exploration and application:
$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Incorporating more information and structure:}`$
One promising direction is to enrich our models by incorporating
additional information and structure. This could involve integrating
data from other sources, such as environmental variables or bird
migration patterns, to further enhance the accuracy of species
annotation aggregation methods.
$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Scaling up to larger datasets:}`$ While our
current research is based on a specific dataset, expanding our models to
handle larger datasets covering a wider geographic region is a natural
progression. This scalability will be crucial for real-world
applications, such as monitoring bird populations on a global scale.
$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Combining modeling with active learning:}`$ An
innovative approach is to integrate our models into a website or mobile
app for bird enthusiasts. By collecting new data through active
learning, we can automatically identify and prioritize challenging cases
for annotation based on our model results. This dynamic feedback loop
will not only improve the accuracy of our annotations but also engage
citizen scientists in species identification research.
$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Applications to other types of ecological data:}`$
Although we focus in this article on bird sound annotations, the same
models could generally be applied to any kind of multi-label crowd
sourcing scenario. For example eBird, iNaturalist and Zooniverse are
examples of global platforms collecting both audio and image
observations of birds and other animals or different environmental
subjects, which are annotated and confirmed by other users. The data
quality questions can be a major challenge in citizen science projects,
but when addressed correctly, citizen science projects can produce
reliable data with high scientific importance ([Munson et al.,
2010](#bib.bib46); [Bonney et al., 2014](#bib.bib45); [Balázs et al.,
2021](#bib.bib47)). By improving the aggregation of partially
disagreeing annotations, our method can thus help to produce meaningful
ecological data in large scale.

In conclusion, our study has advanced the state-of-the-art in bird
species annotation aggregation by leveraging Bayesian hierarchical
models and addressing the complexities of annotator expertise and
species dependencies. We provided a robust framework for improving the
accuracy of citizen science-based bird sound identification, with
implications for ecological studies and conservation efforts. Looking
ahead, our ongoing efforts will focus on enhancing model robustness,
expanding applicability to diverse datasets, and integrating real-time
learning strategies to empower stakeholders in avian research.

## Authors’ contribution

Haoxuan Wang, Patrik Lauha, and David Dunson jointly designed the
method. Haoxuan Wang implemented the methodology and analyses and took
the lead in writing the paper. Patrik Lauha recognized the need for the
proposed methodology for addressing measurement errors in species
classification, and gave substantial feedback on the methods and
results. David Dunson took the lead in conceptualizing the modeling
framework and providing feedback on initial results and the writing. All
authors contributed critically to the drafts and approved the final
version for publication.

## Supplementary Material

## Appendix A Posterior computation

For simplicity, in this section, we assume that there are no missing
values in our identification data
$`\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 12850\left\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\right\}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}`$,
as we can conveniently handle any missing values in R by setting na.rm =
TRUE.

### A.1 Posterior inference for Base Model

#### A.1.1 A list of model parameters to be sampled

In Base, model parameters to be sampled include:

- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  where $`\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29035}`$ is the occurrence
  probability of bird species $`\mathchar 29035`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  where $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$ is the True Positive Rate
  (TPR) of annotator $`\mathchar 29034`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  where $`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}`$ is the False Positive Rate
  (FPR) of annotator $`\mathchar 29034`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\right\delimiter 84054785`$,
  where
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\right\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  is the latent binary variable for audio recording $`\mathchar 29033`$.

\hat{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}}\over\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}}\mathchar 8235\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}^{\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}}}}
``` |  |

is the parameter of
$`\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$’s
full conditional distribution, and
$`\bm{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785}`$
contains all elements in $`\bm{\mathchar 29017}`$ except
$`\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$.
Therefore, we sampled each
$`\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$,
$`\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}`$,
$`\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}`$
from its full conditional:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12824\text{Bernoulli}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314`$ |  | (26) |

### A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM

#### A.2.1 A list of model parameters to be sampled

In DP-BMM introduced in Section
[2.3](#S2.SS3 "2.3 Bernoulli mixture models ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[2.4](#S2.SS4 "2.4 Dirichlet process extension ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
model parameters to be sampled include:

- •
  $`\mathchar 28941`$ is the concentration parameter in the Dirichlet
  process (DP);
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  is the collection of the $`\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}`$ audio
  recordings’ assignments, where $`\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}`$
  is the assignment of recording $`\mathchar 29033`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  where $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$ is the True Positive Rate
  (TPR) of annotator $`\mathchar 29034`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  where $`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}`$ is the False Positive Rate
  (FPR) of annotator $`\mathchar 29034`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\right\delimiter 84054785`$,
  where
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\right\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  is the latent binary variable for audio recording $`\mathchar 29033`$.

#### A.2.2 The posterior distribution

As we have mentioned in Section
[2](#S2 "2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
we integrated out mixing coefficients
$`\bm{\mathchar 28953}\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 28953_{\mathchar 29042}\right\}_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 561}`$.
Additionally, we integrated out parameters
$`\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12349\left\{\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29042}\right\}_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 561}`$.
Here
$`\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
represents the occurrence probabilities of the
$`\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}`$ bird species within the
$`\mathchar 29042^{\text{th}}`$ mixture component, and only sample the
remaining model parameters listed above. This technique not only
simplifies the model but also results in faster convergence of the
Markov chain to its stationary distribution in most cases ([Blei and
Jordan, 2006](#bib.bib22)).

Then, the posterior distribution is as follows:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12906\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785`$ |  | (27) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12906\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}\right\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 8706\left\{\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\right\}\mathchar 8706\left\{\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\right\}\mathchar 314`$ |  |  |

As we can see from Equation
([27](#A1.E27 "In A.2.2 The posterior distribution ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")),
we do not give the explicit form of the integration, which we will
address while sampling
$`\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
and
$`\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785`$.

#### A.2.3 The sampling algorithm

$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Sampling }\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12346`$

The full conditional of $`\mathchar 28941`$ is:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785`$ |  | (28) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785}_{\text{the first term}}\mathchar 8706\underbrace{\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785}_{\text{the second term}}\mathchar 314`$ |  |  |

As seen in Equation
([28](#A1.E28 "In A.2.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")),
the primary challenge in deriving the full conditional of
$`\mathchar 28941`$ lies in obtaining the explicit form of the first
term.

We have formally defined the DP in Equation
([2.4](#S2.SS4 "2.4 Dirichlet process extension ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")),
but there’s still the matter of constructing and representing it. One
commonly used constructive definition for the DP is the stick-breaking
process ([Sethuraman, 1994](#bib.bib24)). For more comprehensive
insights, please refer to [Murphy (2012)](#bib.bib25); [Gelman et al.
(2013)](#bib.bib13).

However, it’s problematic to deal with a countably infinite number of
sticks in practice. Consequently, we will delve into an alternative
approach, namely the Chinese restaurant process (CRP) ([Aldous et al.,
1985](#bib.bib26); [Griffiths et al., 2003](#bib.bib20)). The CRP offers
us an effective way to construct a DP.

Assuming we have the assignments of the
$`\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}`$ audio recordings
$`\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
which correspond to $`\mathchar 29010`$ distinct Bernoulli mixture
components. As presented in Equation
([7](#S2.E7 "In 2.4 Dirichlet process extension ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")),
if
$`\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12824\mathchar 28999`$
represents $`\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}`$ observations from
$`\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12824\text{DP}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29000\delimiter 84054785`$,
generated by $`\mathchar 29010`$ distinct parameters
$`\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29042}`$, then by the definition of
the DP, the predictive distribution can be expressed as:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\bm{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29000\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28941\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 29000\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29010}\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 28942_{\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29042}}\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891`$ |  | (29) |

where $`\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29042}`$ is the number of audio
recordings generated by the parameter
$`\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29042}`$ or assigned to the
$`\mathchar 29042^{\text{th}}`$ mixture component. This predictive
distribution is also known as Blackwell-MacQueen sampling scheme
([Blackwell and MacQueen, 1973](#bib.bib28)). Utilizing it, we could
easily obtain:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28941\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\left\delimiter 67273472\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29042^{\mathchar 8707}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29010}\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29042}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\left\delimiter 67273472\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29042\right\delimiter 84054785`$ |  | (30) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\left\{\begin{aligned} &{{\mathchar 29038_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}}\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 24891\ \ \ \text{if}\ \tilde{\mathchar 29042}\ \text{exists}\mathchar 24891\\
&{{\mathchar 28941\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 24891\ \ \ \text{if}\ \tilde{\mathchar 29042}\ \text{is new}\mathchar 24891\\
\end{aligned}\right\mathchar 314`$ |  |  |

where
$`\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785`$
represents the indicator function, and
$`\mathchar 29042^{\mathchar 8707}`$ denotes a new Bernoulli component
that is outside the existing $`\mathchar 29010`$ mixture components.
This predictive distribution, expressed in terms of assignments
$`\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
is the Chinese restaurant process. It draws an analogy to the seemingly
infinite supply of tables at certain Chinese restaurants ([Murphy,
2012](#bib.bib25)).

Based on the predictive distribution presented in Equation
([30](#A1.E30 "In A.2.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")),
we obtain the explicit form of the first term in Equation
([27](#A1.E27 "In A.2.2 The posterior distribution ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"))
as follows:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785`$ |  | (31) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 29010}{{\mathchar 28672\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 28672\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24609`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 29010}{{\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24609\over\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 24891`$ |  |  |

where
$`\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
and
$`\mathchar 28672\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785`$
is the Gamma function. It is worth mentioning that this probability
distribution over sequences of assignments of mixture components doesn’t
depend on the specific order. This further implies that the assignments
$`\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}`$
is exchangeable by de Finetti’s theorem.

Now we can put the first term and the second term in Equation
([28](#A1.E28 "In A.2.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"))
together, and explicitly express the concentration parameter
$`\mathchar 28941`$’s full conditional as follows:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785`$ |  | (32) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 29010}\over\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\exp\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 29010\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\over\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785}}\exp\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314`$ |  |  |

As direct sampling of $`\mathchar 28941`$ from its full conditional is
not feasible, we opted for an adaptive Metropolis algorithm to update
it. Adaptive Metropolis was firstly proposed in [Haario et al.
(2001)](#bib.bib27). The covariance matrix of the proposal distribution
has a fixed scaling parameters:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 314\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28728^{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 24891
``` |  | (33) |

where $`\mathchar 29028`$ is the the dimension of the parameter.

Specifically, the proposal distribution for $`\mathchar 28941`$ is:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathchar 29041\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314
``` |  | (34) |

In our context, where $`\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721`$,
the optimal acceptance rate is relatively higher, approximately around
$`\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28724`$.
Consequently, in order to update $`\mathchar 28941`$ at each iteration,
we adopted a more direct adaptive approach targeting the optimal
acceptance rate of
$`\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28724`$.

In practice, we can consider $`\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720`$
iterations as a batch, and increases or decreases
$`\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28941}`$ according to the proportion of
accepted proposals within these $`\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720`$
iterations. To explore the space of suitable values more effectively,
it’s convenient to work within the logarithmic scale. As a result, if
the proportion of accepted values for $`\mathchar 28941`$ exceeds
$`\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28724`$, we will
increment
$`\log\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28941}\delimiter 84054785`$
by
$`\min\left\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 28721\over\sqrt{\mathchar 29044}}}\right\}`$,
where $`\mathchar 29044`$ represents the number of iterations.
Conversely, if the proportion of accepted values for $`\mathchar 28941`$
falls below
$`\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28724`$, we will
decrement
$`\log\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28941}\delimiter 84054785`$
by
$`\min\left\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 28721\over\sqrt{\mathchar 29044}}}\right\}`$.

Based on the full conditional as well as the proposal distribution, the
acceptance rate for $`\mathchar 28941`$ is:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 28939\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\delimiter 84054785`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\min\left\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29041\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29041\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\delimiter 84054785}}\right\}`$ |  | (35) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\min\left\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785}}\right\}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\min\left\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\left\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\over\mathchar 28941}}\right\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29010\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\exp\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\left\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28941\over\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 8707}}}\right\delimiter 84054785\right\}\mathchar 314`$ |  |  |

$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Sampling }\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12346`$

In this part, we demonstrate the sequential update process for each
$`\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}`$,
$`\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}`$.

The full conditional of $`\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}`$ is:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}\mathchar 24635\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 28960}\delimiter 84054785`$ |  | (37) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}\right\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785}_{\text{the first term}}\mathchar 8706\underbrace{\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}\right\delimiter 84054785}_{\text{the second term}}`$ |  |  |

For the first term in Equation
([37](#A1.E37 "In A.2.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")),
we can leverage the exchangeability of
$`\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
implied by Equation
([31](#A1.E31 "In A.2.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")).
This will lead to the following expression:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\left\{\begin{aligned} &{{\mathchar 29038_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\ \ \ \text{if}\ \tilde{\mathchar 29042}\ \text{exists}\mathchar 24891\\
&{{\mathchar 28941\over\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\ \ \ \text{if}\ \tilde{\mathchar 29042}\ \text{is new}\mathchar 24891\\
\end{aligned}\right\mathchar 314`$ |  | (38) |

where
$`\mathchar 29038_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}`$
represents the number of audio recordings assigned to the

For the second term,
$`\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}`$ and
$`\bm{\mathchar 29017}`$ are given, since we want to derive the full
conditional distribution of $`\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}`$. This
leads to:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}`$ |  | (39) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29007}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\delimiter 84054785\left\{\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}}\delimiter 84054785\right\}\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}}`$ |  |  |

|  | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 12847`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4946\nolimits_{\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 28721}\text{Bernoulli}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29039_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\text{Beta}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29039_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8235\delimiter 84054785}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026_{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\delimiter 84054785}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29039_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785`$ |  |  |

where
$`\bm{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785`$,
$`\mathchar 29038^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8235\delimiter 84054785}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}`$
represents the cardinality of the set
$`\{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\}`$
and
$`\mathchar 29038^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\delimiter 84054785}_{\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}`$
represents the cardinality of the set
$`\{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\}`$.
Here
$`\mathchar 28994\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785`$
is defined as follows:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathchar 28994\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28939\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28940\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28672\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28939\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28672\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28940\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 28672\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28939\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28940\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 24891
``` |  | (40) |

where
$`\mathchar 28672\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785`$
is the Gamma function we’ve used when deriving the full conditional of
the concentration parameter $`\mathchar 28941`$.

Now, combining the first term in Equation
([38](#A1.E38 "In A.2.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"))
with the second term in Equation
([39](#A1.E39 "In A.2.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")),
we obtain the explicit form of $`\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}`$’s
full condition as follows:

### A.3 Posterior inference for DP-BMM-Hierarchical

Please note that we will not provide a detailed derivation of the
posterior computation for Base-Hierarchical. Instead, we will focus on
demonstrating how to sample model parameters in DP-BMM-Hierarchical. The
key distinction between these two models lies in whether we assume
independence in bird species occurrence. Therefore, to sample parameters
from their joint posterior distribution in Base-Hierarchical, you can
simply combine the sampling algorithm outlined in this section with the
one presented in Section
[A.1.3](#A1.SS1.SSS3 "A.1.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.1 Posterior inference for Base Model ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").

Also, for the sake of clarification, within this subsection, we are
exclusively considering a scenario in which each annotator has the
capacity to identify all $`\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}`$ bird
species at least partly by their vocalizations. In practical
application, for
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$’s
and
$`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$’s
that fall outside the lists mentioned in Section
[2.5](#S2.SS5 "2.5 Hierarchical modeling of annotator expertise ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
we can designate them as NA. Handling these NA values in R is
straightforward, as we can simply set na.rm = TRUE.

#### A.3.1 A list of model parameters to be sampled

In DP-BMM-Hierarchical introduced in Section
[2.5](#S2.SS5 "2.5 Hierarchical modeling of annotator expertise ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
model parameters to be sampled include:

- •
  $`\mathchar 28941`$ is the concentration parameter in the DP;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29050}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  is the collection of the $`\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}`$ audio
  recordings’ assignments, where $`\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}`$
  is the assignment of recording $`\mathchar 29033`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785`$,
  where
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29049}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  is the latent binary variable for recording $`\mathchar 29033`$;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  where $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$ represents annotator
  $`\mathchar 29034`$’s bird song identification expertise when birds
  are actually present in recordings;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28675}\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}`$,
  where
  $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$
  represents annotator $`\mathchar 29034`$’s identification expertise
  for species $`\mathchar 29035`$ when birds are actually present in
  recordings;
- •
  For
  $`\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}`$,
  $`\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}`$,
  $`\mathchar 29015_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 24891\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949\mathchar 24891\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785}\right\}`$
  is the collection of Pólya-Gamma variables introduced due to the
  presence of the two terms:
  $`\mathchar 28955\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785`$
  and
  $`\mathchar 28955\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785`$,
  in the annotation process of DP-BMM-Hierarchical;
- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  where $`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}`$ represents annotator
  $`\mathchar 29034`$’s bird song identification expertise when birds

- •
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28681}\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}`$,
  where
  $`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$
  represents annotator $`\mathchar 29034`$’s identification expertise
  for species $`\mathchar 29035`$ when birds are not present in
  recordings;
- •
  For
  $`\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}`$,
  $`\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}`$,
  $`\mathchar 29015_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960\mathchar 24891\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}^{\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960\mathchar 24891\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785}\right\}`$
  is the collection of Pólya-Gamma variables introduced due to the
  presence of the two terms:
  $`\mathchar 28955\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785`$
  and
  $`\mathchar 28955\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785`$,
  in the annotation process of DP-BMM-Hierarchical;

#### A.3.2 The posterior distribution

As demonstrated in Section
[A.2](#A1.SS2 "A.2 Posterior inference for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
for DP-BMM-Hierarchical, we integrated out mixing coefficients
$`\bm{\mathchar 28953}\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 28953_{\mathchar 29042}\right\}_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 561}`$
as well as
$`\bm{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 12349\left\{\bm{\mathchar 29039}_{\mathchar 29042}\right\}_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 561}`$,
and only sample the remaining parameters listed above.

as follows:
````
