Similar to the procedure for DP-BMM, the next step is to calculate the
product in Equation
([57](#A1.E57 "In A.3.3 The sampling algorithm ‣ A.3 Posterior inference for DP-BMM-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix A Posterior computation ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"))
for both
$`\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720`$
and
$`\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721`$.
Afterwards, we normalized them, and obtained the probability of
$`\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721`$,
denoted as
$`\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$,
$`\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}`$,
$`\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}`$.

Finally, we sampled each
$`\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$ from
its full conditional:

$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Sampling }\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12346`$

As mentioned in Section
[2.5](#S2.SS5 "2.5 Hierarchical modeling of annotator expertise ‣ 2 Materials and methods ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and outlined in our list of model parameters for DP-BMM-Hierarchical, it
should be noted that both $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$ and
$`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}`$ have different meanings compared
to their usage in DP-BMM and Base. In the following parts, we will
provide a detailed explanation of how to sample
$`\bm{\mathchar 28949}`$, $`\bm{\mathchar 28675}`$,
$`\bm{\mathchar 28960}`$ and $`\bm{\mathchar 28681}`$ from their full
conditional distributions. We will particularly focus on how the
introduction of Pólya-Gamma variables allows us to implement a
straightforward Gibbs sampler, even in the presence of the challenging
logistic term.

$`\mathchar 8494\ \textbf{Sampling }\bm{\mathchar 28675}\mathchar 12346`$

Due to the presence of the logistic term, we cannot directly sample each
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$ from
its full conditional, whereas we can jointly sample
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$ as
well as the corresponding Pólya-Gamma variables.

Specifically, for each
$`\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\left\{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}\}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}\}\right\}`$,
assuming that all variables except for
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$ are
given, we applied Pólya-Gamma Augmentation ([Polson et al.,
2013](#bib.bib19); [Donner and Opper, 2018](#bib.bib29)) as follows:

## Appendix B Additional results for simulation studies

Here we provide additional results for simulation studies when different
priors are adopted. Just like we’ve shown in Section
[3.1](#S3.SS1 "3.1 Simulation results ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
all MSEs are in units of
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723}`$ and
\#Anns represents the number of annotations for each recording.
Additionally, in our experiments, we found that when the number of
annotations per recording is relatively low, our models’ performances in
terms of annotation aggregation and estimation of annotators’
identification expertise would be less than satisfactory if we reduced
the sample size of the occurrence probabilities’ prior.

### B.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise

Here we provide our models’ performances in terms of inferring
annotators’ TPRs in Tables
[7](#A2.T7 "Table 7 ‣ B.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ Appendix B Additional results for simulation studies ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[8](#A2.T8 "Table 8 ‣ B.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ Appendix B Additional results for simulation studies ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
For the sake of completeness, we also provide the coverages as well as
the MSEs of annotators’ FPRs of our models in Tables
[9](#A2.T9 "Table 9 ‣ B.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ Appendix B Additional results for simulation studies ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[10](#A2.T10 "Table 10 ‣ B.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ Appendix B Additional results for simulation studies ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
Though our models could achieve relatively high coverages and low MSEs
of annotators’ TPRs, they didn’t do well in inferring annotators’ FPRs.

## Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data

In addition to computing the Gelman-Rubin statistics for model
parameters, we assessed the convergence of the MCMC scheme by examining
traceplots of model parameters across chains. As briefly discussed in
Section
[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
our specific focus is on the following parameters:

- •
  Base: the occurrence probabilities of different species
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  the TPRs of annotators’
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  and the FPRs of annotators’
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29006\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$;
- •
  Base-Hierarchical: the occurrence probabilities of different species
  $`\bm{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29039_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  the annotator-specific parameters of our annotators
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  as well as
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  and the species-specific parameters of the annotators
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28675}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785`$
  as well as
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28681}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785`$;
- •
  DP-BMM: the concentration parameter $`\mathchar 28941`$, the TPRs of
  annotators’
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  and the FPRs of annotators’
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29006\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$;
- •
  DP-BMM-Hierarchical: the concentration parameter $`\mathchar 28941`$,
  the annotator-specific parameters of our annotators
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28949}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$
  as well as
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29012}`$,
  and the species-specific parameters of the annotators
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28675}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785`$
  as well as
  $`\bm{\mathchar 28681}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785`$.

The traceplots for the parameters of interest in each model are
presented separately in Section
[C.1](#A3.SS1 "C.1 Diagnostics for Base ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
Section
[C.2](#A3.SS2 "C.2 Diagnostics for Base-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
Section
[C.3](#A3.SS3 "C.3 Diagnostics for DP-BMM ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
and Section
[C.4](#A3.SS4 "C.4 Diagnostics for DP-BMM-Hierarchial ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
To improve clarity and visualization, we display the traceplots for
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720`$
iterations out of all the iterations. Additionally, for simplicity, we
use the taxon ID in our dataset to reference the bird species. We also
provide the bird species that are included in our analysis along with
their taxonomic information in Appendix
[E](#A5 "Appendix E List of bird species included in our analysis ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").

### C.1 Diagnostics for Base

The traceplots for the occurrence probabilities of nine randomly
selected bird species are presented in Figure
[5](#A3.F5 "Figure 5 ‣ C.1 Diagnostics for Base ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
Additionally, the traceplots for the TPRs and the FPRs of nine randomly
chosen annotators are shown in Figures
[6](#A3.F6 "Figure 6 ‣ C.1 Diagnostics for Base ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[7](#A3.F7 "Figure 7 ‣ C.1 Diagnostics for Base ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
respectively. In general, there is a strong indication of favorable
overall convergence and mixing, with the MCMC samples concentrating
around similar values.

Figure 5: Traceplots for the occurrence probabilities of nine randomly
chosen birds in Base. Colors correspond to different MCMC chains.

Figure 6: Traceplots for the true positive rate of nine randomly chosen
annotators in Base. Colors correspond to different MCMC chains.

Figure 7: Traceplots for the false positive rate of nine randomly chosen
annotators in Base. Colors correspond to different MCMC chains.

### C.2 Diagnostics for Base-Hierarchical

The traceplots for the occurrence probabilities of nine randomly chosen
bird species are shown in Figure
[8](#A3.F8 "Figure 8 ‣ C.2 Diagnostics for Base-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").
As for the parameters related to annotators’ bird song identification
expertise, the traceplots for $`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034}`$’s
and $`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}`$’s of nine randomly chosen
annotators are shown in Figures
[9](#A3.F9 "Figure 9 ‣ C.2 Diagnostics for Base-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[10](#A3.F10 "Figure 10 ‣ C.2 Diagnostics for Base-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
respectively. In addition, the traceplots for three randomly chosen
annotators’
$`\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$ and
$`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}`$ on
three randomly selected species are shown in Figures
[11](#A3.F11 "Figure 11 ‣ C.2 Diagnostics for Base-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies")
and
[12](#A3.F12 "Figure 12 ‣ C.2 Diagnostics for Base-Hierarchical ‣ Appendix C MCMC diagnostics for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
respectively. In general, though some parameters exhibit a certain
degree of autocorrelation, the Markov chains have converged sufficiently
well and mixing seems to be good.

## Appendix D Additional results for application to species annotation data

Similar to the discussions in Section
[3.2.2](#S3.SS2.SSS2 "3.2.2 Assessment of sound identification expertise ‣ 3.2 Results for bird species annotations ‣ 3 Results ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
because of the convergence of Markov chains and the resemblance of
posterior distributions obtained from different Markov chains for each
model, here we present the posterior distribution of each annotator’s
$`\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29034}`$ acquired from one chain for each
model in Figures
[21](#A4.F21 "Figure 21 ‣ Appendix D Additional results for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
[22](#A4.F22 "Figure 22 ‣ Appendix D Additional results for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
[23](#A4.F23 "Figure 23 ‣ Appendix D Additional results for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies"),
[24](#A4.F24 "Figure 24 ‣ Appendix D Additional results for application to species annotation data ‣ Bayesian modeling of multi-species labeling errors in ecological studies").

Figure 21: The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in Base.
Levels
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724`$
represent the different levels of annotators’ bird song identification
expertise. Level $`\mathchar 28724`$ corresponds to the highest skill
level, followed by decreasing levels of 3, 2, and 1 in that order.

Figure 22: The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in
Base-Hierarchical. Levels
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724`$
represent the different levels of annotators’ bird song identification
expertise. Level $`\mathchar 28724`$ corresponds to the highest skill
level, followed by decreasing levels of 3, 2, and 1 in that order.

Figure 23: The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in DP-BMM.
Levels
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724`$
represent the different levels of annotators’ bird song identification
expertise. Level $`\mathchar 28724`$ corresponds to the highest skill
level, followed by decreasing levels of 3, 2, and 1 in that order.

Figure 24: The posterior distributions of annotators’ TPRs in
DP-BMM-Hierarchical. Levels
$`\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724`$
represent the different levels of annotators’ bird song identification
